I find it really hard to follow through on instructions and actually finish my tasks or chores. It's getting to the point where it's really affecting my daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have these attention problems been present since childhood, or did they start more recently? If they started recently, was there a particular stress, depression, or anxiety around that time?\n\nPatient: They've definitely been present since childhood, even before I turned 12. However, they've become a much bigger, more noticeable struggle for about the last year and seven months.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often feel restless or fidgety, have trouble staying seated, talk excessively, or act impulsively? Or are your difficulties mostly with attention and organization?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel restless, fidgety, or talk excessively. My struggles are almost entirely with attention and organization, especially with following through on instructions and avoiding tasks that require sustained mental effort. I also find myself frequently forgetting simple daily chores and errands.\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and organization problems cause significant difficulties in more than one setting, such as both at work or school and at home or in relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely, it affects almost every part of my life. At work, I really struggle to finish tasks and follow through on projects, and at home, my chores often get left half-done. I'm also constantly forgetting personal things like returning calls, paying bills on time, and keeping my appointments.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often make careless mistakes, lose things you need, get easily distracted, or have trouble listening when someone speaks directly to you?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I don't really experience those specific issues. I don't think I have much trouble with losing things or making careless mistakes, and I can usually listen fine when someone is talking directly to me.\n\nDoctor: When you need to do something that takes a lot of mental effort, do you mainly put it off or avoid starting it, or do you start but quickly lose focus and leave it unfinished?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly, I struggle with both of those. I tend to actively avoid or put off starting tasks that require a lot of sustained mental effort because they feel so overwhelming. But even when I do manage to start them, I almost always lose focus and fail to follow through, leaving them unfinished.

But even when I do manage to start them, I almost always lose focus and fail to follow through, leaving them unfinished.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child, did parents or teachers notice these attention and organization problems, such as incomplete schoolwork, being described as forgetful or not following through, or needing repeated reminders?\n\nPatient: Yes, my parents and teachers noticed it constantly when I was growing up. They also frequently complained about how incredibly forgetful I was, even back before I turned 12."

But even when I do manage to start them, I almost always lose focus and fail to follow through, leaving them unfinished.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child, did parents or teachers notice these attention and organization problems, such as incomplete schoolwork, being described as forgetful or not following through, or needing repeated reminders?\n\nPatient: Yes, my parents and teachers noticed it constantly when I was growing up. They were always pointing out how I struggled to follow through on instructions and rarely finished my schoolwork. They also frequently complained about how incredibly forgetful I was, even back before I turned 12.\n\nDoctor: Even when a task is important and you’ve started it, do you often have trouble sustaining your attention long enough to finish it?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, definitely. Even when I start an important task, I struggle to stay focused and follow through to the end. I often leave work projects or chores half-finished because my mind just drifts away.

Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Instruction_Follow through_Difficulty\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(No match in profile or inference only)\nAnswer strategy: State that you don't really have significant trouble with organizing tasks or managing time specifically, or that you haven't noticed this being a major issue for you."
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I'm here today because I've been struggling a lot with focusing and paying attention. I find it really hard to follow through on instructions and actually finish my tasks or chores. It's getting to the point where it's really affecting my daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have these attention problems been present since childhood, or did they start more recently? If they started recently, was there a particular stress, depression, or anxiety around that time?\n\nPatient: They've definitely been present since childhood, even before I turned 12. However, they've become a much bigger, more noticeable struggle for about the last year and seven months.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often feel restless or fidgety, have trouble staying seated, talk excessively, or act impulsively? Or are your difficulties mostly with attention and organization?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel restless, fidgety, or talk excessively. My struggles are almost entirely with attention and organization, especially with following through on instructions and avoiding tasks that require sustained mental effort. I also find myself frequently forgetting simple daily chores and errands.\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and organization problems cause significant difficulties in more than one setting, such as both at work or school and at home or in relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely, it affects almost every part of my life. At work, I really struggle to finish tasks and follow through on projects, and at home, my chores often get left half-done. I'm also constantly forgetting personal things like returning calls, paying bills on time, and keeping my appointments.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often make careless mistakes, lose things you need, get easily distracted, or have trouble listening when someone speaks directly to you?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I don't really experience those specific issues. I don't think I have much trouble with losing things or making careless mistakes, and I can usually listen fine when someone is talking directly to me.\n\nDoctor: When you need to do something that takes a lot of mental effort, do you mainly put it off or avoid starting it, or do you start but quickly lose focus and leave it unfinished?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I struggle with both of those. I tend to actively avoid or put off starting tasks that require a lot of sustained mental effort because they feel so overwhelming. But even when I do manage to start them, I almost always lose focus and fail to follow through, leaving them unfinished.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child, did parents or teachers notice these attention and organization problems, such as incomplete schoolwork, being described as forgetful or not following through, or needing repeated reminders?\n\nPatient: Yes, my parents and teachers noticed it constantly when I was growing up. They were always pointing out how I struggled to follow through on instructions and rarely finished my schoolwork. They also frequently complained about how incredibly forgetful I was, even back before I turned 12.\n\nDoctor: Even when a task is important and you’ve started it, do you often have trouble sustaining your attention long enough to finish it?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. Even when I start an important task, I struggle to stay focused and follow through to the end. I often leave work projects or chores half-finished because my mind just drifts away.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble organizing tasks and activities, like keeping things in order, planning steps, or managing your time?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven't really noticed organization or managing my time being a major issue for me. My main struggle is more about actually starting the tasks and following through to finish them, rather than planning them out or keeping things in order.

I often leave work projects or chores half-finished because my mind just drifts away.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble organizing tasks and activities, like keeping things in order, planning steps, or managing your time?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed organization or managing my time being a major issue for me. My main struggle is more about actually starting the tasks and following through to finish them, rather than planning them out or keeping things in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget routine daily activities, like appointments, returning calls, paying bills, or doing chores, even when you intended to do them?\n\nPatient: Yes, I frequently struggle with that. I often completely forget to pay my bills on time, miss scheduled appointments, or neglect daily chores, even when I had every intention of doing them. It's like those tasks just completely slip my mind until it's too late."
  }

Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.
